```python
    dup_hits = [h for h in hits if (h or "").startswith("duplication.")]
    if dup_hits:
        row["labels"]["duplicate-not-reuse"] = "; ".join(x for x in [row["labels"].get("duplicate-not-reuse"), "AIV: " + ", ".join(dup_hits)] if x)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- write + summarize
Path(ARGS.out_jsonl).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
with open(ARGS.out_jsonl, "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
    for row in sorted(rows.values(), key=lambda r: r["pr"]):
        fh.write(json.dumps(row, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")


def pct(a, b):
    return f"{100 * a / b:.1f}%" if b else "n/a"


def stats(group):
    dec = [r for r in group if r["outcome"] in ("merged", "closed")]
    m = sum(r["outcome"] == "merged" for r in dec)
    return len(group), len(dec), m


tier_rows = {"A": [r for r in rows.values() if "A" in r["tiers"]], "B": [r for r in rows.values() if "B" in r["tiers"]]}
md = ["# Airflow PR pattern labels (stored corpus)", "",
      "Generated by `benchmarks/airflow/scripts/label-pr-slop-patterns.py` from data already in the benchmark "
      "repository. Labels describe the change, not the author. Heuristic labels (tier B) are candidates for review.", ""]
for tier, name in (("A", "index, 15,575 PRs: title, body, size, outcome"), ("B", "stored patches"), ):
    n, dec, m = stats(tier_rows[tier])
    human = [r for r in tier_rows[tier] if not r["mechanical"]]
    md.append(f"- Tier {tier} ({name}): {n} PRs, {dec} decided, baseline merge rate {pct(m, dec)}; "
              f"{len(human)} not bot/backport/release (`human`).")
md.append(f"- Tier C (real AIV on checkouts): {census_n} PRs; all findings: "
          f"{', '.join(f'{k} {v}' for k, v in census_rules.most_common()) or 'none'}.")
md += ["", "| Label | Tier | Meaning | PRs (all) | PRs (human) | Share of tier (human) | Merge rate when labeled (human) | Baseline (human) | AI-flagged share (human) |",
       "|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|"]
summary = {}
for lid, name, tier, meaning in LABELS:
    if tier == "-":
        md.append(f"| {lid} `{name}` | none | {meaning} | not measurable | | | | | |")
        summary[name] = None
        continue
    pool = tier_rows[tier]
    hpool = [r for r in pool if not r["mechanical"]]
    lab = [r for r in pool if name in r["labels"]]
    hlab = [r for r in hpool if name in r["labels"]]
    _, hdec, hm = stats(hlab)
    _, bdec, bm = stats(hpool)
    ai_share = pct(sum(r["ai"] for r in hlab), len(hlab))
    md.append(f"| {lid} `{name}` | {tier} | {meaning} | {len(lab)} | {len(hlab)} | {pct(len(hlab), len(hpool))} | "
              f"{pct(hm, hdec)} | {pct(bm, bdec)} | {ai_share} |")
    summary[name] = {"all": len(lab), "human": len(hlab), "human_decided": hdec, "human_merged": hm,
                     "human_pool": len(hpool), "human_pool_decided": bdec, "human_pool_merged": bm}

md += ["", "AI-flagged share in the human pool: tier A "
       f"{pct(sum(r['ai'] for r in tier_rows['A'] if not r['mechanical']), sum(not r['mechanical'] for r in tier_rows['A']))}, tier B "
       f"{pct(sum(r['ai'] for r in tier_rows['B'] if not r['mechanical']), sum(not r['mechanical'] for r in tier_rows['B']))}.", ""]

# per sub-signal merge rate (tier B, human PRs)
hb = [r for r in tier_rows["B"] if not r["mechanical"]]
_, bdec_b, bm_b = stats(hb)
md += ["## Sub-signals (tier B, human PRs)", "",
       f"Baseline merge rate {pct(bm_b, bdec_b)}. Below baseline means the signal is more common in PRs that were not merged.", "",
       "| Label | Sub-signal | PRs | Merge rate when present | AI-flagged share |", "|---|---|---:|---:|---:|"]
subs = defaultdict(list)
for r in hb:
    for label, keys in (r.get("signals") or {}).items():
        for k in keys:
            subs[(label, k)].append(r)
for (label, k), group in sorted(subs.items(), key=lambda x: (x[0][0], -len(x[1]))):
    _, d_, m_ = stats(group)
    md.append(f"| `{label}` | {k} | {len(group)} | {pct(m_, d_)} | {pct(sum(r['ai'] for r in group), len(group))} |")
    summary[f"{label}/{k}"] = {"prs": len(group), "decided": d_, "merged": m_}

# size thresholds on tier A (human)
ha = [r for r in tier_rows["A"] if not r["mechanical"]]
md += ["", "## Size bands (tier A, human PRs)", "", "| Files changed | PRs | Merge rate | Small-change title: PRs | Small-change title: merge rate |",
       "|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|"]
for lo, hi in ((1, 1), (2, 5), (6, 19), (20, 49), (50, 99), (100, 999), (1000, 10 ** 9)):
    band = [r for r in ha if lo <= r["files"] <= hi]
    sm = [r for r in band if small_intent(r["title"])]
    _, d1, m1 = stats(band)
    _, d2, m2 = stats(sm)
    md.append(f"| {lo}{'+' if hi > 10 ** 6 else ('' if lo == hi else f' to {hi}')} | {len(band)} | {pct(m1, d1)} | {len(sm)} | {pct(m2, d2)} |")

co = Counter()
for r in rows.values():
    if r["mechanical"]:
        continue
    ks = sorted(r["labels"])
    co[len(ks)] += 1
md += ["", "## Labels per PR (human, all tiers)", "", ", ".join(f"{k} labels: {v}" for k, v in sorted(co.items())), ""]

md += ["## Examples (human PRs, largest first; check before quoting)", ""]
for lid, name, tier, _ in LABELS:
    if tier == "-":
        continue
    ex = sorted([r for r in rows.values() if name in r["labels"] and not r["mechanical"]], key=lambda r: -r["lines"])[:6]
    if ex:
        md.append(f"### {lid} `{name}`")
        md.append("")
        for r in ex:
            md.append(f"- [#{r['pr']}]({r['url']}) {r['outcome']}{' AI' if r['ai'] else ''}: {r['title'][:90]} ({r['labels'][name][:140]})")
        md.append("")
md += ["## What the data cannot show", "",
       "- `commit-churn` needs per-commit file lists; the corpus stores only the final diff.",
       "- Hallucinated APIs and reuse of existing helpers need the repository at the PR base. Tier C (real AIV "
       "on checkouts) covers this for 627 PRs; tier B only sees code duplicated inside the PR.",
       "- Patches are not stored for every PR, and very large PRs are under-represented in tier B.",
       ""]
Path(ARGS.out_md).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
Path(ARGS.out_md).write_text("\n".join(md) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
print("\n".join(md[:60]))
```

### `benchmarks/airflow/scripts/mine-maintainer-pain-themes.py`

Component J: maintainer-pain theme index. 69 lines, sha256 `bf064d6370d4bfc2`.

```python
# Copyright 2026 Vaquar Khan
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""Count maintainer pain themes in the stored Airflow dev-list threads and issue comments.

Keyword counts are a coarse index for finding threads to read; the analysis in
docs/AI-integration/MAINTAINER-PAIN-AND-PR-PATTERNS-ANALYSIS.md is based on reading the threads.

    python benchmarks/airflow/scripts/mine-maintainer-pain-themes.py [--bench ../aiv-airflow-bench] [--out <dir>]
"""
import argparse
import json
import re
from collections import Counter, defaultdict
from pathlib import Path

_ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
_ap.add_argument("--bench", default=str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[4] / "aiv-airflow-bench"))
_ap.add_argument("--out", default=None)
ARGS = _ap.parse_args()
C = Path(ARGS.bench) / "corpus"
THEMES = {
    "size / split": r"\b(too (big|large)|huge (pr|change|diff)|split (it|this|the pr|into)|smaller (prs?|chunks|pieces)|massive (pr|change)|hundreds of (files|lines)|\d{2,} files)\b",
    "unrelated / scope": r"\b(unrelated (change|file)s?|out of scope|scope creep|not related to|drive-by|unnecessary changes?)\b",
    "duplicate / reinvent": r"\b(already exists?|already (have|has|implemented)|duplicat(e|es|ed|ion)|reinvent|same as existing|there is already)\b",
    "not run / not tested": r"\b(did(n't| not) (run|test)|not tested|untested|doesn't (compile|work|run)|tests? (fail|are failing|broke)|ci (fails|is red))\b",
    "hallucination": r"\b(hallucinat\w*|does not exist|doesn't exist|no such (method|function|module|api|class)|made[- ]up|invented)\b",
    "no understanding / no engagement": r"\b(can you explain|do you understand|did you (read|review)|copy[- ]?past(e|ed)|not respond\w*|no response|ghost\w*|abandon\w*)\b",
    "volume / maintainer time": r"\b(waste of (time|review)|reviewer time|maintainers?'? time|burden|flood\w*|spam\w*|overwhelm\w*|burn ?out|too many prs|volume)\b",
    "description mismatch": r"\b(description (does not|doesn't) match|pr description|misleading|claims? to fix|doesn't fix|does not fix)\b",
    "disclosure / policy": r"\b(generated-by|disclos\w*|ai policy|gen-?ai|asf policy|co-authored-by)\b",
    "slop wording": r"\b(ai slop|slop|low[- ]quality|llm[- ]generated|chatgpt|ai[- ]generated)\b",
}
RX = {k: re.compile(v, re.I) for k, v in THEMES.items()}


def texts():
    for f in (C / "ai-year" / "mail-threads").glob("*.json"):
        t = json.loads(f.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        for m in t.get("messages", []):
            body = "\n".join(l for l in (m.get("body") or "").split("\n") if not l.lstrip().startswith(">"))
            yield "mail", t.get("subject"), m.get("date"), body
    for f in (C / "ai-year" / "issues").glob("*.json"):
        t = json.loads(f.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        iss = t.get("issue", {})
        for c in t.get("comments", []):
            yield "issue-comment", f"#{iss.get('number')} {iss.get('title')}", c.get("created_at"), c.get("body") or ""


hits = Counter()
docs = Counter()
snips = defaultdict(list)
n = 0
for kind, subj, date, body in texts():
    n += 1
    docs[kind] += 1
    for k, rx in RX.items():
        m = rx.search(body)
        if m:
            hits[(kind, k)] += 1
            if len(snips[k]) < 8:
                s = max(0, m.start() - 160)
                snips[k].append(f"[{kind} {str(date)[:10]} | {str(subj)[:60]}] ..." + re.sub(r"\s+", " ", body[s:m.end() + 160]) + "...")
print("documents:", dict(docs))
for k in THEMES:
    print(f"{k:34} mail={hits[('mail', k)]:4} ({100*hits[('mail', k)]/max(1,docs['mail']):4.1f}%)  "
          f"issue-comments={hits[('issue-comment', k)]:4} ({100*hits[('issue-comment', k)]/max(1,docs['issue-comment']):4.1f}%)")
if ARGS.out:
    Path(ARGS.out).mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    (Path(ARGS.out) / "pain-snippets.json").write_text(json.dumps(snips, indent=1, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")
```

### `scripts/e2e-change-policy.py`

Component J: end-to-end check of the gate through the real jar. 138 lines, sha256 `fdfe891bf07c0592`.

```python
# Copyright 2026 Vaquar Khan
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""End-to-end checks of the opt-in change-policy gate through the real CLI jar and real Git repos.

Build the CLI first (mvn -pl aiv-cli -am package -DskipTests), then run:

    python scripts/e2e-change-policy.py

Prints one JSON line per scenario and ALL E2E OK on success; exits 1 otherwise. PR metadata is passed
the way the composite action passes it (AIV_PR_TITLE, AIV_PR_BODY, AIV_PR_LABELS,
AIV_PR_AUTHOR_ASSOCIATION).
"""
import json
import os
import shutil
import subprocess
import tempfile
from pathlib import Path

GATE = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
JAR = GATE / "aiv-cli" / "target" / "aiv-cli-1.0.4.jar"

BASE = """schema_version: 1
gates:
  - id: density
    enabled: false
  - id: design
    enabled: false
  - id: dependency
    enabled: false
  - id: cohesion
    enabled: false
  - id: test-adequacy
    enabled: false
  - id: duplication
    enabled: false
{extra}
"""
POLICY = """  - id: change-policy
    enabled: true
    config:
      pack: {pack}
{body}
"""
PR_ENV = {"AIV_PR_TITLE": "Fix typo in scheduler", "AIV_PR_BODY": "Added 12 test cases for the loop.",
          "AIV_PR_AUTHOR_ASSOCIATION": "CONTRIBUTOR", "AIV_PR_LABELS": "[]"}


def git(repo, *args):
    subprocess.run(["git", "-c", "user.name=t", "-c", "user.email=t@example.com", "-c", "commit.gpgsign=false", *args],
                   cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)


def big_untested_change(repo):
    for i in range(12):
        (repo / "src" / f"m{i}.py").write_text("".join(f"v{j} = {j}\n" for j in range(25)), encoding="utf-8")
    (repo / ".github" / "workflows").mkdir(parents=True)
    (repo / ".github" / "workflows" / "ci.yml").write_text("on: push\n", encoding="utf-8")


def mass_change(repo):
    for i in range(1000):
        (repo / "src" / f"gen_{i}.py").write_text(f"v = {i}\n", encoding="utf-8")


def run(name, policy, env=None, message="Fix typo", change=None):
    repo = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="aiv-cp-e2e-"))
    try:
        git(repo, "init", "-q", "-b", "main")
        (repo / ".aiv").mkdir()
        (repo / ".aiv" / "config.yaml").write_text(policy, encoding="utf-8")
        (repo / "src").mkdir()
        (repo / "src" / "app.py").write_text("x = 1\n", encoding="utf-8")
        git(repo, "add", "-A")
        git(repo, "commit", "-q", "-m", "base")
        base = subprocess.run(["git", "rev-parse", "HEAD"], cwd=repo, capture_output=True, text=True).stdout.strip()
        (change or big_untested_change)(repo)
        git(repo, "add", "-A")
        git(repo, "commit", "-q", "-m", message)
        out = repo / "report.json"
        clean_env = {k: v for k, v in os.environ.items() if not k.startswith("AIV_PR_")}
        p = subprocess.run(["java", "-jar", str(JAR), "--workspace", str(repo), "--diff", base, "--head", "HEAD",
                            "--require-trusted-config", "--quiet", "--output-json", str(out)],
                           capture_output=True, text=True, encoding="utf-8", env={**clean_env, **(env or {})})
        rep = json.loads(out.read_text(encoding="utf-8")) if out.exists() else {}
        rules = sorted({(g["partition"], f["rule_id"]) for g in rep.get("gates", []) for f in g.get("findings", [])
                        if g["id"] == "change-policy"})
        msg = next((g.get("message", "") for g in rep.get("gates", []) if g["id"] == "change-policy"), "")
        res = {"exit": p.returncode, "rules": rules, "message": msg[:300],
               "stderr_tail": p.stderr[-300:] if p.returncode == 2 else ""}
        print(name, json.dumps(res))
        return res
    finally:
        shutil.rmtree(repo, ignore_errors=True)


def policy(pack="oss-maintainer", body=""):
    return BASE.format(extra=POLICY.format(pack=pack, body=body))


FAIL_UNTESTED = """      overrides:
        per_policy: { untested-source-change: [tests-not-applicable] }
      policies:
        - { id: untested-source-change, action: fail }"""

checks = [
    ("gate not configured: default off, no change-policy findings", run("1", BASE.format(extra=""), PR_ENV),
     lambda r: r["exit"] == 0 and r["rules"] == []),
    ("pack oss-maintainer with PR metadata: advisory findings, exit 0", run("2", policy(), PR_ENV),
     lambda r: r["exit"] == 0 and {("advisory", "policy.untested-source-change"), ("advisory", "policy.small-intent-large-diff"),
                                    ("advisory", "policy.infra-with-source"), ("advisory", "policy.ci-change-by-non-collaborator"),
                                    ("advisory", "policy.description-claims-mismatch")} <= set(r["rules"])),
    ("local run without PR metadata: metadata policies skipped, not faked", run("3", policy(), {}),
     lambda r: r["exit"] == 0 and set(r["rules"]) == {("advisory", "policy.untested-source-change"),
                                                       ("advisory", "policy.infra-with-source")}),
    ("promoted policy blocks; the other policies stay advisory", run("4", policy(body=FAIL_UNTESTED), PR_ENV),
     lambda r: r["exit"] == 1 and [x for x in r["rules"] if x[0] == "blocking"] == [("blocking", "policy.untested-source-change")]
     and ("advisory", "policy.infra-with-source") in r["rules"]),
    ("per-policy override label turns the block into a notice", run("5", policy(body=FAIL_UNTESTED),
                                                                    {**PR_ENV, "AIV_PR_LABELS": '["tests-not-applicable"]'}),
     lambda r: r["exit"] == 0 and any(x[1] == "change-policy.override" for x in r["rules"])
     and not any(x[0] == "blocking" for x in r["rules"])),
    ("airflow pack reads real git history: conventional-commit subject", run("6", policy("airflow"), PR_ENV, "fix: typo"),
     lambda r: r["exit"] == 0 and ("advisory", "policy.commit-hygiene") in r["rules"]),
    ("maintainer author: non-collaborator CI policy does not fire", run("7", policy(), {**PR_ENV, "AIV_PR_AUTHOR_ASSOCIATION": "MEMBER"}),
     lambda r: r["exit"] == 0 and not any("ci-change-by-non-collaborator" in x[1] for x in r["rules"])),
    ("1,000-file diff: mega-diff blocks by default", run("9", policy(), PR_ENV, change=mass_change),
     lambda r: r["exit"] == 1 and ("blocking", "policy.mega-diff") in r["rules"]),
    ("1,000-file diff with a maintainer override label: notice, exit 0",
     run("10", policy(body="      overrides: { labels: [mass-change-ok] }"), {**PR_ENV, "AIV_PR_LABELS": '["mass-change-ok"]'},
         change=mass_change),
     lambda r: r["exit"] == 0 and any(x[1] == "change-policy.override" for x in r["rules"])),
    ("invalid policy config: exit 2, never silent", run("8", policy(body="      policies: [ { id: x, when: { fact: nope, gt: 1 } } ]"), PR_ENV),
     lambda r: r["exit"] == 2 and "unknown fact" in r["stderr_tail"]),
]
bad = [name for name, res, ok in checks if not ok(res)]
print("ALL E2E OK" if not bad else f"E2E FAILED: {bad}")
raise SystemExit(1 if bad else 0)
```

### `docs/AI-integration/CHANGE-POLICY-ENGINE-DESIGN.md`

Component J: design, decisions, calibration. 434 lines, sha256 `d616986ce5c93bb8`.

````markdown
# Change policy engine: design (v2)

Status: **implemented (phase 1)** in `aiv-plugin-change-policy`, default off, calibrated on 1,991 stored
PRs (section 14) and the full 15,575-PR Airflow index (section 15). Not yet built: reuse facts (4.6),
phase 2 (4.7, section 6), `git.unlinked_author_commits`.
Author: Viquar Khan.

Goal: let a project state, in one versioned YAML file, what a reviewable pull request looks like
(size budget by author and area, tests for source changes, description consistent with the diff,
template and commit rules), and have AIV enforce it deterministically before a maintainer spends time
on it.

v2 changes are driven by [MAINTAINER-PAIN-AND-PR-PATTERNS-ANALYSIS.md](MAINTAINER-PAIN-AND-PR-PATTERNS-ANALYSIS.md):
`mixed-concerns` is removed (it fires on 24.5% of merged PRs), reuse findings are advisory only, and
seven policies are added for pain that maintainers actually raised.

## 1. Problem and evidence (short form)

- Review capacity is the bottleneck. Airflow's contributor queue went from about 260 to about 870 PRs in
  2026; maintainers say 80 to 90% of triage is deterministic.
- Size only matters at the extreme: merge rate is flat up to 99 files and 46.2% at 100+ files, but 92 of
  those 199 PRs were merged. Size is a budget with classification and an override, never a blanket block.
- The strongest patch signal is source change without tests (lift 2.13, merge rate 43.0% vs 61.7%).
- Mixed file categories, intra-PR clones and duplicated function names are **more** common in merged PRs.
- Maintainers raised fabricated descriptions (22 tests claimed, 7 test lines), missing "why", template
  non-compliance, commit hygiene, CI changes from outsiders and untested provider changes.

Full numbers, caveats and sources are in the analysis document.

## 2. Principles

- **Facts, then policy.** Collectors compute deterministic facts. Policies are declarative conditions
  over facts. No policy runs code or calls a model.
- **Same input, same answer.** Facts come only from pinned base and head commits, the protected policy,
  and PR metadata supplied by CI.
- **Opt-in and calibrated.** Gate default-off. Every pack policy starts at `warn`. A policy moves to
  `fail` only after it flags at most the project's merged-PR ceiling (suggested 2%) and a maintainer
  records the decision (section 9).
- **Explainable and quiet.** Each finding names the policy, facts and thresholds, and how to fix or
  override. Output goes into the existing single AIV comment and fix brief, never a new bot thread
  (maintainer pain P13).
- **No authorship claims.** Policies describe changes, never who or what wrote them.
- **Per-PR only.** Queue-level and people-level questions (author PR volume, duplicate PRs for one
  issue, engagement, reputation) stay with Magpie and GitHub (section 10).

## 3. Architecture

```text
             trusted policy (.aiv/change-policy.yaml from the base ref, packs from the jar)
                                        |
Diff + git + workspace + PR metadata --> Fact collectors --> Facts (JSON, deterministic)
                                        |                     |
                       other gates' rule hits (phase 2)       v
                                        +------------> Policy evaluator --> findings policy.<id>
                                                              |                 |
                                                      risk score (optional)   rule_actions, overrides
                                                              v                 v
                                                     labels / routing     JSON, SARIF, PR comment, fix brief
```

New module `aiv-plugin-change-policy`, gate id `change-policy`, default-off. Reuse facts come from a
shared `aiv-analysis` library also used by the `duplication` gate. Existing `cohesion` and
`duplication` behaviour and the benchmark baseline do not change.

## 4. Facts

Every fact has a stable name, type and definition. Collectors are pure functions of the diff, git
objects for base and head, the head tree and metadata. A fact that cannot be computed in this run is
`unknown` (section 5).

### 4.1 File classification (first; every other fact can filter on it)

Each changed path gets one **category** from ordered globs (pack defaults, project overrides):
`source`, `test`, `docs`, `config`, `ci`, `build-infra`, `dependency-manifest`, `lockfile`, `generated`,
`vendored`, `migration`, `ai-instruction`, `binary`. Each path also gets an **area** from the project's
`areas` map (for example `providers`, `core`, `ui`, `chart`), falling back to the first `area_depth`
directory segments.

Change kinds: `added`, `modified`, `deleted`, `renamed`, `rename-only` (similarity 100%),
`mode-only`, `whitespace-only` (empty with `git diff -w`).

A file is **reviewable** when its category is not in `non_reviewable_categories` (default `lockfile`,
`generated`, `vendored`, `binary`) and its kind is not `rename-only`, `mode-only` or `whitespace-only`.

Test detection is per ecosystem. The pack ships globs for Python (`tests/**`, `test_*.py`), Java
(`src/test/**`, `*Test.java`, `*IT.java`), Go (`*_test.go`), JS/TS (`*.test.*`, `*.spec.*`,
`__tests__/**`), and projects add their own (for example Airflow UI tests or provider system tests).
The analysis showed UI `.tsx` changes misread as untested without this.

### 4.2 Size and scope

| Fact | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `files.changed`, `files.reviewable` | all changed paths; reviewable subset |
| `files.added`, `files.deleted`, `files.renamed` | by change kind |
| `files.by_category.<cat>`, `files.by_area.<area>` | counts |
| `lines.changed`, `lines.added`, `lines.deleted` | numstat over reviewable files |
| `lines.source_changed`, `lines.test_changed`, `lines.docs_changed` | by category |
| `lines.non_test_changed` | reviewable lines excluding test files (the Airflow "excluding tests" budget) |
| `lines.total_changed` | numstat over every changed path, including generated and vendored files (raw diff size) |
| `code.large_new_files` | new source files with 500+ added lines |
| `code.max_function_lines` | longest run of added lines after a new function start (Python, Go, JS/TS, Kotlin, Rust), upper bound |
| `code.stub_functions` | new functions whose first statement is `pass`, `...`, `raise NotImplementedError`, `todo!()` and similar |
| `scope.areas`, `scope.area_list` | distinct areas over reviewable files |
| `churn.whitespace_only_files` | files whose only change is formatting |
| `churn.max_file_share` | largest share of `lines.changed` in one file |

### 4.3 Metadata (from CI; `unknown` in local runs)

| Fact | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `intent.class` | title against the pack's intent map: `typo`, `docs`, `fix`, `bump`, `test`, `refactor`, `feature`, `unknown` |
| `meta.labels` | PR labels at evaluation time |
| `meta.author_association` | GitHub value: `OWNER`, `MEMBER`, `COLLABORATOR`, `CONTRIBUTOR`, `FIRST_TIME_CONTRIBUTOR`, `FIRST_TIMER`, `NONE` |
| `meta.author_tier` | project mapping of association, default `maintainer` (OWNER, MEMBER, COLLABORATOR) or `external` |
| `meta.is_draft` | PR is a draft |
| `meta.linked_issue` | body or title matches a configured pattern (default `#\d+` with `fixes`/`closes`/`related`; projects may add Jira-style keys) |
| `meta.body_words` | words in the body outside template boilerplate and HTML comments |
| `template.sections_missing` | required headings (from config) that are absent or empty |
| `claims.tests_count` | largest number of tests the body says were **added** ("added N tests", "N new tests", "N tests were added"); counts of tests run do not count |
| `claims.tests_added` | body says tests were added or updated |
| `claims.tests_overclaim` | `claims.tests_count - tests.functions_added` when a count is claimed, else 0 |
| `claims.ai_disclosure_mismatch` | a filled `Generated-by:` trailer while the "Yes" disclosure box is unchecked |

### 4.4 Git history (from the checkout; requires enough fetch depth)

| Fact | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `git.commits` | commits in `base..head` |
| `git.behind_base` | commits on the base branch not in head (`git rev-list --count head..base`) |
| `git.commits_without_body` | commits whose message has only a subject line |
| `git.conventional_prefix_commits` | subjects matching a configured prefix pattern (default `^(feat|fix|chore|docs|refactor|test|ci)(\(.+\))?!?:`) |
| `git.unlinked_author_commits` | commits whose author email matches a configured unlinked pattern (for example no `noreply.github.com` and not in the allow list); GitHub-side linkage is not checked |

If the clone is too shallow to compute a git fact, it is `unknown` and the facts note says which depth is
needed. It never fails closed on depth alone.

### 4.5 Tests

| Fact | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `tests.files_changed` | test files changed |
| `tests.functions_added` | added test function or method definitions (per-ecosystem patterns: `def test_`, `@Test`, `func Test`, `it(`/`test(`) |
| `tests.ratio` | `lines.test_changed / max(1, lines.source_changed)` |
| `tests.source_areas_without_tests` | areas with 1+ source line changed and no test file changed in the same area |

### 4.6 Reuse (shared `aiv-analysis` library, advisory only)

| Fact | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `reuse.exact_clones` | added blocks of `min_lines` normalized lines that already exist elsewhere in the repo |
| `reuse.renamed_clones` | same, after identifiers and literals are normalized (Type-2) |
| `reuse.test_clones` | added test bodies that match an existing test body (maintainer pain P9) |
| `reuse.reinvented_functions` | new non-test functions whose normalized body matches an existing function |
| `reuse.parallel_modules` | new files whose name matches an existing util or helper module |

Intra-PR clones and duplicated function names inside the PR are **not** facts in v2. They were more
common in merged PRs (analysis section 3.2).

### 4.7 Other gates (phase 2)

`hits.<rule-id-glob>`: count of findings from gates that already ran (for example `hits.invariant.*`).
Needs the orchestrator post-pass in section 7.3.

## 5. Policy language

YAML with structured conditions only. `all`, `any`, `not`, and leaf comparisons on a fact with `eq`,
`ne`, `gt`, `gte`, `lt`, `lte`, `in`, `not_in`, `contains`. `budgets` give per-tier and per-area
limits without writing one policy per combination.

```yaml
# config of the change-policy gate entry in .aiv/config.yaml (pinned from the protected base ref)
pack: oss-maintainer
classification:
  generated: ["**/generated/**", "**/*_pb2.py", "**/*.min.js"]
  build-infra: ["dev/breeze/**", "scripts/ci/**"]
  test: ["airflow-core/src/airflow/ui/**/*.test.tsx", "providers/*/tests/system/**"]
areas:
  providers: ["providers/**"]
  core: ["airflow-core/src/**"]
  ui: ["airflow-core/src/airflow/ui/**"]
budgets:                     # most specific match wins: tier+area, then tier, then default
  default:  { files: 50, lines: 1500 }
  external: { files: 30, lines: 800 }
  external.core: { non_test_lines: 200 }
template:
  required_sections: ["Why", "How was this tested"]
overrides:
  labels: [large-change-approved]
  per_policy: { untested-source-change: [tests-not-applicable] }
policies:
  - id: over-budget
    when: { fact: budget.exceeded, eq: true }
    action: warn
    overridable: true
    message: "{budget.detail} for {meta.author_tier} PRs in {scope.area_list}. Split the PR, or ask a
              maintainer to apply large-change-approved."
```

Pack policies (all `warn` by default):

| Policy | Condition (pack default) | Pain | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `over-budget` | any applicable budget exceeded | P12 | 100+ files merge 46.2% |
| `small-intent-large-diff` | `intent.class` in typo, docs, fix, bump and `files.reviewable > 10` | P10, P12 | 49.6% vs 70.0% merged; lift 1.36 |
| `untested-source-change` | `lines.source_changed >= 200` and `tests.files_changed == 0`; variant per area via `tests.source_areas_without_tests` | P8 | lift 2.13, merge 43.0% |
| `infra-with-source` | `files.by_category.ci >= 1` and `lines.source_changed > 0` (CI workflows only, section 14) | P17 | merge 51.9% |
| `ci-change-by-non-collaborator` | `meta.author_tier == external` and ci or build-infra files changed | P17 | Airflow "your PR, your CI" proposal |
| `lockfile-change` | lockfile changed without a dependency manifest change, or `intent.class` not `bump` | P10 | merge 51.0% |
| `formatting-churn` | `churn.whitespace_only_files >= 3` | P10 | merge 51.7% (heuristic) |
| `description-claims-mismatch` | `claims.tests_overclaim > 2` (claimed added tests minus added test functions), or `claims.tests_added`, no test files and source changed | P7 | lift 4.03, 0.4% of merged (section 14) |
| `template-compliance` | author is not a maintainer and (`template.sections_missing > 0` or `meta.body_words < 15`) | P6, P14 | lift 3.33 for the text rule |
| `mega-diff` (**fail**) | `files.changed >= 1000` | P12 | all 27 such Airflow PRs closed unmerged (section 15) |
| `small-fix-huge-diff` | small-change title and `lines.total_changed >= 5000` | P10, P12 | 32.3% merged vs 75.9% baseline (human PRs) |
| `large-new-code-unit` | a new 500+ line source file, a 150+ line new function, or 2+ stub functions | scaffolding | 47.1% merged when flagged vs 61.7% |
| `disclosure-consistency` | `claims.ai_disclosure_mismatch == true` (tool named in `Generated-by:`, box unchecked) | P16 | fabricated case had box unchecked |
| `commit-hygiene` | conventional prefixes or commits without body or unlinked authors above configured counts | P20 | proposed by Ash Berlin-Taylor |
| `behind-base` | `git.behind_base > 200` | CI readiness | examples 648 and 207 behind |
| `reused-code` | `reuse.reinvented_functions >= 1` or `reuse.renamed_clones >= 2` or `reuse.test_clones >= 1`; **advisory, cannot be promoted in the pack** | P9 | research; not raised by Airflow maintainers |

Language rules:

- Unknown facts, operators or params are configuration errors (exit 2).
- A fact `unknown` in this run makes the leaf `unknown`. A policy with an unknown leaf does not fire and
  is listed in the facts note, so local runs never fake a PR check.
- `action` is `fail`, `warn` or `off`. Existing `rule_actions` and `unsuppressible_rules` apply to
  `policy.<id>`.
- Messages reference facts and params with `{name}`; values are sanitised.

## 6. Risk score and labels (optional)

```yaml
risk:
  weights: { over-budget: 3, untested-source-change: 2, small-intent-large-diff: 2,
             description-claims-mismatch: 2, ci-change-by-non-collaborator: 2, template-compliance: 1,
             "hits.invariant.*": 3 }
  label_at: 4            # label the PR (for example aiv:needs-attention)
  fail_at: null          # null: the score never blocks on its own
```

Sum of weights of fired policies, reported with its parts. It feeds labels and the existing
`review-routing` tiers (score at or above `label_at` gives tier `none`, so no AI review is spent before a
human looks). Weights for policies with lift below 1 are rejected at load time when calibration data is
present (section 9).

## 7. Runtime integration

### 7.1 Inputs

- Diff, numstat, change types: already in `Diff` and `ChangedFile`.
- `git diff -w --numstat`, `git rev-list --count`, `git log --format` for base..head: new calls in
  `GitDiffProvider` under existing timeout and fail-closed rules (depth issues become `unknown`).
- Head tree for reuse: the duplication gate's bounded workspace walk.
- PR metadata: existing `AIV_PR_TITLE`, `AIV_PR_BODY`; new `AIV_PR_LABELS` (JSON array),
  `AIV_PR_AUTHOR_ASSOCIATION`, `AIV_PR_DRAFT`, all set by the Action from the event payload.

### 7.2 Overrides and trust

- Policy is read from the base ref (`--require-trusted-config`), so a PR cannot raise its own budget or
  reclassify its files.
- Override labels come from the event. Applying labels needs triage permission on GitHub. Projects that
  need more set `overridable: false` and use branch-protection bypass.
- Every override becomes a notice (`[POLICY-OVERRIDE] over-budget by label large-change-approved`) and an
  audit-log entry. The review ledger reports override rate per policy; a high rate means the policy is
  miscalibrated.

### 7.3 Orchestrator phase 2

Optional `PostGate` interface: runs after regular gates and receives their results, used for `hits.*`.
Without it, all facts in 4.1 to 4.6 still work.

### 7.4 Outputs

- Findings `policy.<id>` in JSON and SARIF, file `(diff)` or the specific file.
- One note `change-policy.facts` with the full fact snapshot.
- `aiv facts --diff <base> --head <head>` prints facts without evaluating policies.
- Fix brief sections: "How to split this PR" (over-budget, small-intent-large-diff) listing reviewable
  files by area; "Tests expected" (untested-source-change) listing source areas without tests.

## 8. Built-in packs

| Pack | Purpose | Defaults |
|---|---|---|
| `oss-maintainer` | general OSS | policies in section 5 at `warn`; budgets default 50 files / 1,500 lines, external 30 / 800 |
| `airflow` | tuned on the Airflow corpus | areas (providers, core, ui, chart), build-infra paths, UI and system-test globs, thresholds from calibration |
| `strict-agent` | repos that accept agent PRs only for small tasks | `over-budget`, `small-intent-large-diff` and `untested-source-change` at `fail`, overridable |

Packs ship in the jar and are versioned with the CLI.

## 9. Calibration and evaluation

| Dataset | Facts available | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| Airflow index, 15,575 PRs | files, lines, title, outcome | size budgets and intent; merged flag rate |
| 1,991 stored patches (Airflow plus 6 projects) | classification, tests, churn, claims from body | per-policy merged flag rate, lift, merge rate when flagged |
| Selected heads with checkout | everything incl. git and reuse | git and reuse facts, end-to-end verdicts |
| Designed fixtures in the labeled suite | everything | recall per policy, plus clean controls (big mechanical bump, rename-only refactor, feature with tests and docs) |

Known gap: the stored corpus has no `author_association`, so tier budgets cannot be calibrated from it
yet. The collector script will be extended to fetch it; until then tier budgets ship as documented
examples, not pack defaults.

For each policy the report states merged flag rate with a 95% interval, closed flag rate, lift, merge
rate when flagged, and fixture recall with exact bounds. Promotion to `fail` needs: fixture recall lower
bound at least 90%, merged flag rate under the project ceiling (suggested 2%), lift above 1, and a
recorded maintainer decision. A policy whose measured lift is at or below 1 is reported as
"not discriminating" and cannot be promoted.

Not claimed: a percentage of all AI slop caught, or that a flagged PR is AI-written.

## 10. What AIV does not do (Magpie or GitHub)

| Need | Owner |
|---|---|
| Open PRs per author, PRs per week | GitHub PR cap (Airflow adopted 5), Magpie stats |
| Several PRs for one issue | Magpie triage across the queue |
| Engagement, reputation, author response time | Magpie |
| Whether the "why" is coherent, whether tests are meaningful | human or model review (Magpie, Copilot after AIV) |
| Queue ranking and reviewer assignment | Magpie, CODEOWNERS |

AIV's role is the deterministic per-PR part that maintainers say is 80 to 90% of triage, with output
that Magpie can read (`change-policy.facts`, labels, fix brief).

## 11. Rollout

1. **Observe:** gate with facts only; publish `aiv facts` and the calibration report.
2. **Warn:** `oss-maintainer` at `warn`; findings in the AIV comment and fix brief.
3. **Enforce:** projects promote measured policies to `fail` with override labels.
4. **Phase 2:** `PostGate`, `hits.*`, risk score, routing integration.

## 12. Implementation plan and tests

| Step | Work | Tests |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Classification, areas, per-ecosystem test globs; size, scope, tests facts | unit tests per fact and ecosystem; UI `.tsx` and provider fixtures |
| 2 | Git facts (`-w` numstat, behind base, commit messages) in `GitDiffProvider` | temp-repo tests; shallow clone gives `unknown`; timeout fail-closed |
| 3 | Metadata facts: intent, template sections, claims, trailer vs checkbox, labels, association | table tests on real PR bodies from the corpus (including the 22-tests case) |
| 4 | Policy loader and evaluator, budgets resolution, unknown handling, messages | table tests per operator and budget precedence, error cases, sanitising |
| 5 | Gate, packs, overrides, notices, outputs, `aiv facts`, fix-brief sections | gate tests, `MainTest` end to end, JSON/SARIF schema tests; 100% line coverage kept |
| 6 | `aiv-analysis` reuse facts; move duplication gate onto it | per-language tests; duplication tests and benchmark unchanged |
| 7 | Calibration script over the corpora; fixtures in the labeled suite | separate denominators; lift and merged flag rate per policy |
| 8 | Docs, commented preset snippets, handoff and source appendix | `validate-docs.py`, appendix `--check` |
| 9 | Phase 2: `PostGate`, `hits.*`, risk score, routing | orchestrator tests; benchmark unchanged when off |

## 13. Decisions taken

1. **Routing stays separate.** `review-routing` keeps its own config and benchmark semantics. Policies
   can feed routing in phase 2 through `hits.*` and the risk score.
2. **Linked issues:** `#123` and GitHub issue URLs always count; projects add tracker keys through
   `linked_issue_patterns` (the Airflow pack does not need Jira keys any more).
3. **Overrides, both kinds:** `overrides.labels` apply to policies marked `overridable: true`;
   `override_labels` and `overrides.per_policy` apply to one policy only. A policy with neither cannot
   be overridden by label. Pack policies `over-budget`, `small-intent-large-diff` and
   `untested-source-change` are overridable.
4. **Budgets:** `oss-maintainer` keeps 50 files and 1,500 lines (the full Airflow index shows the
   merge-rate drop starts above 99 files). Tier budgets ship as documented examples only, because the
   corpus has no author association to calibrate them.
5. **`description-claims-mismatch` ships at `warn`.** Calibration (section 14) gives 0.4% of merged PRs
   flagged and lift 4.03 after two precision fixes.
6. **Config lives in the gate entry** of the pinned `.aiv/config.yaml`, not a second file. It inherits
   trusted-policy loading and `rule_actions` without new plumbing.

## 14. Calibration results (phase 1)

Run with the real gate through `benchmarks/airflow/scripts/run-change-policy-calibration.py`, output in
`benchmarks/airflow/reports/change-policy-calibration-latest.md`. 1,991 PRs with stored patches
(Airflow 1,148 with the `airflow` pack, six other projects with `oss-maintainer`): 1,105 merged and
686 closed unmerged, baseline merge rate 61.7%.

| Policy | Merged flagged | Closed flagged | Lift | Merge rate when flagged | Meets promotion test |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| `untested-source-change` | 3.2% | 7.4% | 2.35 | 40.7% | no, merged rate above 2% |
| `description-claims-mismatch` | 0.4% | 1.5% | 4.03 | 28.6% | yes |
| `small-intent-large-diff` | 1.7% | 2.2% | 1.27 | 55.9% | yes |
| `infra-with-source` | 1.1% | 1.3% | 1.21 | 57.1% | yes |
| `lockfile-change` | 0.3% | 0.4% | 1.61 | 50.0% | yes (6 PRs, too few to act on) |
| `formatting-churn` | 0.0% | 0.3% | n/a | 0.0% | yes (2 PRs, too few to act on) |
| `over-budget` | 4.3% | 3.1% | 0.72 | 69.1% | no, see note |
| template text rule, any author | 2.8% | 9.3% | 3.33 | 32.6% | no, merged rate above 2% |
| `disclosure-consistency` | 0.1% | 0.0% | n/a | 100% | no, 1 PR |

Notes:

- Any policy fired on 11.9% of merged and 21.4% of closed PRs. All pack policies stay `warn`; a project
  promotes one only after its own measurement.
- `over-budget` looks inverted here because the patch sample rarely stores very large closed PRs. On
  the full 15,575-PR index, 100+ file PRs merge at 46.2% against 70 to 83% below 100 files. It stays a
  budget with an override label, not a detector.
- `template-compliance` exempts maintainers, and the corpus has no author association, so the gate
  skips it; the calibration-only variant without the exemption is the row above.
  `behind-base`, `commit-hygiene` and `ci-change-by-non-collaborator` need git history or author
  association and were skipped on every PR.

Changes made because of calibration (each one is covered by a unit test):

1. Test-count claims count only tests **added** ("added 22 test cases"), not tests run ("127 tests
   passing", "1111 passed"). Merged flag rate went from 3.0% to 0.4%.
2. Offers ("happy to add tests", "will add tests") are not claims; `claims.tests_added` also requires
   source changes.
3. `disclosure-consistency` fires only for "tool named, box unchecked". A ticked box with the tool
   named inline was the normal Airflow answer and made the first version inverted (11.1% merged).
   Template placeholders `[Tool Name...]` are ignored.
4. `infra-with-source` uses CI workflow files only. Build-infrastructure files (dev tooling and its
   tests) routinely accompany merged source changes in Airflow. Lift went from 0.71 to 1.21.
5. `template-compliance` exempts maintainers: most merged hits were release, backport and CI-upgrade
   PRs with short bodies.
6. Scala, Kotlin and Ruby test file names were added to the default test globs.

## 15. Size and scaffolding policies (added after the PR pattern labeling)

The labeling run (`benchmarks/airflow/scripts/label-pr-slop-patterns.py`, report
`benchmarks/airflow/reports/pr-pattern-labels-latest.md`) showed two size patterns that separate
rejected PRs sharply. Three policies were added and calibrated, now also on the full 15,575-PR Airflow
index (the calibration script has an index tier that feeds real file counts, line totals, titles and
bodies to the gate).

| Policy | Full index, human PRs: merged flagged | Closed flagged | Merge rate when flagged | Stored patches: merge rate when flagged |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `mega-diff` (fail) | 0 of 8,446 | 26 of 2,688 | 0.0% | n/a (none stored) |
| `small-fix-huge-diff` | 10 (0.1%) | 21 (0.8%) | 32.3% | 10.0% (all PRs) |
| `large-new-code-unit` | n/a | n/a | n/a | 47.1% (baseline 61.7%) |

- `mega-diff` is the only blocking pack policy: every one of the 27 Airflow PRs with 1,000+ changed
  files was closed unmerged, including bots and backports. Most were small fixes built on the wrong base.
  It stays overridable by label for real mass changes.
- `small-fix-huge-diff` uses 5,000 lines, not 1,000. The threshold sweep on human PRs gave: 1,000+
  lines 66.1% merged, 2,000+ 56.1%, 3,000+ 53.8%, 5,000+ 32.3%, 10,000+ 15.0% (baseline 75.9%).
- `large-new-code-unit` covers the scaffolding sub-signals that held up in the labeling (new 500+ line
  files, 150+ line functions, stub bodies). Nesting depth and duplication inside the PR were left out:
  they were more common in merged PRs.

With these policies the gate would block 0 merged and 27 closed PRs on the full index (`mega-diff`), and
0 PRs on the stored patches. Labeled, structural and selected-real benchmarks are unchanged
(TP 35, FN 0, FP 0, TN 15, advisories 5/5; structural 2/2 with 0 false blocks; #59957 TP, #70842 TN).
````

### `docs/AI-integration/MAINTAINER-PAIN-AND-PR-PATTERNS-ANALYSIS.md`

Component J: evidence. 270 lines, sha256 `aaf26a9f4a171d89`.

````markdown
# Maintainer pain and PR change patterns: evidence for the change policy engine

Author: Viquar Khan. Status: analysis, input to [CHANGE-POLICY-ENGINE-DESIGN.md](CHANGE-POLICY-ENGINE-DESIGN.md) v2.

This document records what maintainers actually complain about and what the stored PR data says about
each proposed signal. Several signals I expected to work do not separate merged from closed PRs, and
the design was changed because of that.

Data comes from the sibling benchmark repository `aiv-airflow-bench`. Both analyses can be re-run:

```text
python benchmarks/airflow/scripts/mine-change-patterns.py          [--bench ../aiv-airflow-bench] [--out <dir>]
python benchmarks/airflow/scripts/mine-maintainer-pain-themes.py   [--bench ../aiv-airflow-bench] [--out <dir>]
```

## 1. Summary

1. The main maintainer pain is review capacity, not bad code as such. Making a PR is cheap, reviewing
   it is not. Airflow's contributor queue grew from about 260 PRs (January 2026) to about 870
   (September 2026) and fell to about 590 only after a cap of 5 open PRs per non-collaborator.
2. Maintainers say most triage is deterministic: "more than 90%" of problematic PRs (March) and "about
   80%" of triage actions (June) need no model. That is the space AIV can own.
3. Size matters only at the extreme. Merge rate is flat from 1 to 99 files and drops to 46.2% at 100+
   files, yet 92 of those 199 PRs were merged. Size is a review-cost budget, not an AI signal.
4. The strongest patch-level signal is **source change without tests**: 200+ source lines and no test
   lines merge at 43.0% against a 61.7% baseline (closed/merged lift 2.13). This matches the GCP
   "untested provider changes" thread.
5. Three signals I proposed in design v1 are **inverted** (more common in merged PRs) and must not
   block: mixed file categories, intra-PR duplication, and duplicated function names.
6. Reuse of existing helpers is a real research finding but **no Airflow maintainer complained about
   it** in 29 threads. Duplicated *tests* and duplicate *PRs* were raised; reinvented utilities were not.
7. Some pain belongs to Magpie or GitHub, not AIV: per-author volume, duplicate PRs across the queue,
   engagement history, and bot-comment noise.

## 2. What maintainers say (Airflow dev@, 29 threads, November 2025 to October 2026)

Counted only when a person raises the problem themselves. Meeting-note items are note-taker summaries.

| # | Pain | People | Example evidence |
|---|---|---:|---|
| P1 | Review capacity is the bottleneck | 8 | "These days the bottleneck is clearly reviewing PRs and not creating PRs" (Vincent Beck) |
| P2 | Huge, growing queue; ready PRs wait with no comment | 5 | 1,024 open PRs; "anything beyond 200 isn't manageable" (Christos Bisias) |
| P3 | Drive-by AI slop nobody read | 8 | "I'm bored of wasting my time on drive-by AI slop" (Ash Berlin-Taylor) |
| P4 | Bulk PRs from single accounts | 3 | batches of 25 to 50 PRs; top 3 authors held 96 open PRs |
| P5 | Authors vanish or cannot explain the change | 5 | about 60% of triaged PRs closed with no author response |
| P6 | The "why" is missing | 5 | "Agents are very good at producing PRs with no real motivation behind them" (Kaxil Naik) |
| P7 | Description does not match the diff | 1 | description claimed 22 test cases, diff added 7 test lines; PR was 648 commits behind main |
| P8 | Untested provider and live-service changes | 5 | Google provider PRs with only mocked tests; regression in merged #74305 |
| P9 | Duplicated or out-of-place tests | 2 | "same test case already exists somewhere in the test suite" (Przemyslaw Mirowski) |
| P10 | Unrelated file changes bundled in | 2 | "changing unrelated files" named as a predictable agent pattern (Sameer Mesiah) |
| P11 | Several PRs for one issue | 2 | issue #68382 with 7+ PRs; Jarek Potiuk disagrees it is a problem |
| P12 | Large PRs are expensive | 3 | proposal: under 10 lines excluding tests for non-collaborator core PRs |
| P13 | Bot and AI review comment noise | 5 | triage and Copilot comments bury human comments |
| P14 | Agent instructions and templates not followed | 4 | "AI instructions cannot be trusted" (Ash Berlin-Taylor) |
| P15 | Triage false positives | 4 | a critical production fix was drafted by the triage agent |
| P16 | Gen-AI disclosure field unreliable | 3 | field often missing; fabricated case had the box unchecked |
| P17 | Shared CI cost per push | 3 | one workflow hit 40 concurrent jobs against an ASF limit of 20 |
| P18 | Agent commit churn after review | 2 | dozens of review comments answered by agent pushes, then closed |
| P19 | Bloated AI-written docs | 4 | a 100-line troubleshooting doc that could have been 5 lines |
| P20 | Commit hygiene, assignment gaming, prompt injection | 1 each | conventional-commit prefixes, commits without body, unlinked authors |

Adopted mitigations so far: the 5-open-PR cap, the auto-triage "Drafting" process, de-noised triage
comments, and the Gen-AI disclosure checkbox. Proposed: "your PR, your CI" (PRs touching `.github/`,
`dev/breeze/` or `scripts/ci/` stay draft until a maintainer runs project CI), required template
headings checked in CI, commit-message rules, real test evidence for Google provider PRs, and per-area
size caps.

Two lessons for AIV. First, P15: wrong drafting of a legitimate PR costs trust, so every new rule starts
advisory and is measured on merged PRs. Second, P13: AIV output must stay short and must not add a
second noisy bot voice.

## 3. What the PR data says

### 3.1 Size (full index, 15,575 Airflow PRs)

| Files changed | 1 | 2 to 5 | 6 to 19 | 20 to 49 | 50 to 99 | 100+ |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| merge rate | 82.7% | 73.8% | 80.5% | 76.9% | 71.9% | 46.2% |

| Group | 50+ files | 100+ files | 1,000+ lines |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| merged | 1.7% | 0.8% | 6.1% |
| closed unmerged | 4.2% | 3.1% | 15.7% |
| AI disclosed | 2.2% | 1.2% | 9.4% |
| not disclosed | 2.3% | 1.4% | 8.3% |

Titles that promise a small change (fix, typo, bump, minor, docs) and touch 20+ files: 117 PRs, 49.6%
merged against 70.0% for the same titles under 20 files.

### 3.2 Patch-level signals (1,991 PRs with stored patches)

Airflow 1,148 plus curl 241, godot 177, flink 127, ghostty 124, kafka 100, cosmic 74. Decided: 1,105
merged and 686 closed (baseline merge rate 61.7%); 200 open; 699 AI-flagged. Lift is closed rate over
merged rate; above 1 means the signal is more common in rejected PRs.

| Signal | Merged | Closed | Lift | Merge rate when flagged | AI-flagged | Not AI | Verdict |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 200+ source lines, no test lines | 3.3% | 7.1% | 2.13 | 43.0% | 1.9% | 8.2% | **keep, strongest** |
| lockfile changed | 4.5% | 7.0% | 1.55 | 51.0% | 4.9% | 5.1% | keep, advisory |
| CI files plus source | 1.3% | 1.9% | 1.50 | 51.9% | 1.3% | 1.6% | keep, advisory |
| whitespace-only file | 1.4% | 2.0% | 1.50 | 51.7% | 0.9% | 1.8% | keep, advisory |
| small-intent title, 10+ reviewable files | 1.2% | 1.6% | 1.36 | 54.2% | 1.6% | 1.3% | keep |
| 3+ commented-out code lines | 3.2% | 3.8% | 1.20 | 57.4% | 3.0% | 3.8% | weak |
| comment-only source change | 4.3% | 5.0% | 1.17 | 58.0% | 3.7% | 4.9% | weak |
| dependency manifest changed | 10.2% | 9.8% | 0.96 | 62.8% | 7.7% | 10.6% | no signal |
| 1,000+ reviewable lines | 7.3% | 5.2% | 0.72 | 69.2% | 9.6% | 5.0% | inverted in this sample |
| 3+ reviewable file categories | 24.5% | 15.2% | 0.62 | 72.3% | 32.2% | 14.9% | **drop** |
| duplicated function names in PR | 14.7% | 9.0% | 0.62 | 72.3% | 20.7% | 8.7% | **advisory only** |
| intra-PR clone (exact) | 7.1% | 3.8% | 0.53 | 75.2% | 8.6% | 4.5% | **advisory only** |
| intra-PR clone (renamed) 2+ | 8.6% | 5.5% | 0.64 | 71.4% | 11.2% | 5.9% | **advisory only** |
| broad exception handlers 2+ | 1.5% | 0.9% | 0.57 | 73.9% | 2.0% | 0.9% | no signal |

How to read this:

- Mixed categories, duplicated names and intra-PR clones are more common in AI-flagged PRs **and** in
  merged PRs. They describe larger, well-structured changes (a feature with tests, docs and config),
  not rejected ones. Blocking on them would mostly hit good PRs.
- "No tests" is common in non-AI PRs (8.2%) and rare in AI-flagged ones (1.9%). Agents tend to add
  tests. The signal is about review risk, not authorship, which is what we want.
- Small UI changes (`.tsx`) showed up as "source without tests" because their tests live in a
  different layout. Test detection must be per ecosystem.

### 3.3 Caveats

- The patch sample is not random. Very large PRs often have no stored patch, so closed PRs with 50+
  reviewable files appear as 0 here. Use the full index (3.1) for size, and the patch sample only for
  shape signals.
- 63 patches were truncated by GitHub; their facts are lower bounds.
- The whitespace-only heuristic compares sorted added and removed lines, so moved blocks also count.
  The engine will use `git diff -w` instead.
- "Closed" mixes rejected, abandoned and superseded PRs. A signal with lift above 1 is a review-cost
  indicator, not proof of a bad PR.
- AI-flagged means disclosed or labelled. P16 says disclosure is unreliable, so AI versus not-AI
  columns are indicative only.
- Keyword theme counts over 1,519 list messages and 1,292 issue comments (second script) are a reading
  index, not a measurement. Section 2 is based on reading the threads.

## 4. External research

- Each added file lowers the odds a PR gets any review comment by about 8.7%
  ([arXiv 2609.22610](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22610v1)).
- Larger agent PRs merge less often ([arXiv 2602.19441](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.19441v1)).
- Agent PR descriptions often disagree with the diff
  ([arXiv 2601.04886](https://arxiv.org/html/2601.04886v2)), which matches P7.
- Agents leave duplicated logic in 50.8% of task chains by turn 5
  ([arXiv 2609.35357](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35357)).
- Copy and paste rose from 8% to 18% of changed lines while moved code fell below 10%
  ([GitClear](https://gitkraken.gitclear.com/recent_ai_developer_productivity_code_quality_research)).

The research supports reuse detection in general. Our Airflow evidence does not yet show it as a
maintainer pain, so reuse stays advisory until a project measures it.

## 5. Mapping pain to owner

| Pain | AIV (deterministic, per PR) | Magpie or GitHub (queue, people, judgement) |
|---|---|---|
| P1, P2 queue and capacity | routing tier and label so humans spend time on likely-mergeable PRs | queue ranking, CODEOWNERS, review rotation |
| P3, P5, P6 nobody read it, no why | template sections present and non-empty; linked issue | whether the why is coherent; whether the author owns it |
| P4 bulk authors | none | GitHub open-PR cap (adopted), Magpie author stats |
| P7 description mismatch | numeric test claims vs test changes in the diff; "added tests" with no test files | wording and intent mismatch |
| P8 untested changes | `untested-source-change`, per area (providers) | whether tests are meaningful; system-test evidence quality |
| P9 duplicated tests | exact and renamed test-body clones against the suite (advisory) | test placement and quality |
| P10 unrelated changes | files outside the area implied by title or labels (advisory) | confirming the change is unrelated |
| P11 several PRs per issue | none (needs other PRs) | Magpie triage |
| P12 large PRs | budgets by author tier and area, file classification, override label | split decisions |
| P13 bot noise | one short comment, fix brief, labels; no repeated reviews | Copilot policy |
| P14, P16 templates and disclosure | required headings, trailer vs checkbox consistency, instruction-file invariants | model-based compliance |
| P17 CI cost | `ci-change-by-non-collaborator` keeps such PRs advisory-flagged for draft | fork CI ("your PR, your CI") |
| P18 commit churn | none per run | Magpie history |
| P19 docs bloat | docs lines budget (advisory) | value judgement |
| P20 commit hygiene, behind base | commit message rules, unlinked author, commits behind base | none |

## 6. Consequences for the design

Applied in design v2:

- Remove `mixed-concerns`. Replace it with `infra-with-source` and `lockfile-change` (advisory).
- Add `untested-source-change` with per-ecosystem test globs and per-area overrides.
- Add an author tier (`meta.author_association`) and area budgets, so a project can encode "non-collaborator
  core PRs under N lines excluding tests" while collaborators keep a larger budget.
- Add `description-claims-mismatch`, `template-compliance`, `commit-hygiene`, `behind-base` and
  `ci-change-by-non-collaborator`.
- Keep reuse findings advisory, add a test-duplicate variant, and exclude test files from
  `dup_defs`-style name checks.
- Calibrate every policy on merged PRs first: a policy may move to `fail` only if it flags at most 2% of
  merged PRs and a maintainer records the decision.

## 7. Can deterministic signals catch 50% of AI slop?

Measured with `benchmarks/airflow/scripts/measure-slop-signal-recall.py` (report
`benchmarks/airflow/reports/slop-signal-recall-latest.md`). The corpus has no human-labeled slop set,
so the proxy is AI-flagged human Airflow PRs closed unmerged (1,095 PRs). Controls: 3,527 AI-flagged
merged PRs and 8,446 merged human PRs.

| Signals allowed | Merged PRs flagged (budget) | Slop proxy caught |
|---|---:|---:|
| per-PR gate signals only | 2% | 3.3% |
| per-PR gate signals only | 10% | 21.1% |
| per-PR gate signals only | 20% | 30.5% |
| gate plus project history | 2% | 9.2% |
| gate plus project history | 10% | 23.0% |
| gate plus project history | 20% | 43.0% |

Answer: **no, not at an acceptable false-flag rate.** Reaching 43% of the proxy means flagging 1 in 5
merged PRs, and no combination reached 50%.

Why, according to research on agent PRs:

- Most rejections are not code defects. In 562 rejected agent PRs, 38% were abandoned or never reviewed,
  23% were duplicates of another PR, and 17% failed CI or tests
  ([Ehsani et al., arXiv 2601.15195](https://arxiv.org/html/2601.15195)). In 353 rejected agent PRs, only
  35.7% were clear agent failures; 31.2% were workflow reasons and 33.1% had no visible rationale
  ([arXiv 2605.22534](https://arxiv.org/html/2605.22534v1)). Rejected-AI is therefore a loose proxy,
  and a perfect code check would still miss most of it.
- The strongest predictors are outside the diff: CI results, reviewer engagement, author history and
  duplicate PRs ([arXiv 2602.19441](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.19441v1),
  [arXiv 2601.18749](https://arxiv.org/html/2601.18749v1)). Models that reach high accuracy use
  metadata and text features learned per dataset ([arXiv 2607.12057](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12057v1)),
  which is not a deterministic, explainable gate.
- Several signals that "look like AI" (agent-default `## Summary` / `## Test plan` sections, bursts of
  PRs) flag authorship and activity, not low quality: author bursts flag 45.6% of merged PRs.

What the measurement supports:

1. **Per-PR gate (AIV):** high-precision blocks only (`mega-diff`, objective invariants), with the
   size, test and description policies as warnings. This catches a small share of slop with almost no
   false blocks.
2. **Project history (Magpie or a GitHub query):** `poor-track-record` and `no-merge-yet-and-burst` flag
   about 1% of merged PRs each and lift recall most per false flag. `competing-open-pr` and
   `issue-already-fixed` cover the duplicate-PR cause that research ranks second.
3. **After the PR opens:** CI failures and author silence (the top two rejection causes) are deterministic
   but only exist after opening; they belong in triage (Magpie), not in the PR-time gate.

The honest product claim: deterministic checks block objective defects and accidental mega diffs with
near-zero false blocks, and route the rest. They do not catch half of AI slop on their own.

## 8. JEV (model judgment) on Amazon Bedrock, alone and with AIV

Measured with `benchmarks/airflow/scripts/run-jev-bedrock-eval.py` (report
`benchmarks/airflow/reports/jev-bedrock-eval-latest.md`). The real `jev` gate ran against a local
System One adapter backed by Bedrock (`integrations/jev-bedrock-adapter`, Claude Sonnet 4.5,
temperature 0) with six questions: description mismatch, unrelated changes, unfinished code, missing
why, unnecessary code, likely broken. AI-disclosure lines were removed from descriptions. Sample: human
Airflow PRs with stored patches, 118 slop proxy, 100 AI merged, 100 non-AI merged, 60 non-AI closed.
382 calls, about 2.0M input and 0.12M output tokens (roughly $8 at list price).

| Layer (JEV at P(yes) 0.7) | Slop proxy | AI merged | Non-AI merged | Non-AI closed |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| AIV deterministic (gate plus history) | 30.5% | 19.0% | 18.0% | 26.7% |
| JEV alone | 12.7% | 4.0% | 14.0% | 41.7% |
| AIV plus JEV | 41.5% | 23.0% | 26.0% | 55.0% |

At P(yes) 0.9, JEV flags 2.5% of the slop proxy and 0% of merged PRs, so AIV plus JEV gives 32.2% with
about 18 to 19% of merged PRs flagged (all by the deterministic layer).

Findings:

1. **JEV adds about 11 points over AIV**, reaching 41.5% of the slop proxy, but the merged false-flag rate
   rises to about 25%. 50% was not reached.
2. **JEV flags rejected non-AI PRs far more than rejected AI PRs** (41.7% vs 12.7%). Rejected AI PRs look
   clean to the model: complete, motivated, on topic. This matches the "superficial competence" property
   of slop, and the research finding that most rejected agent PRs fail for workflow reasons (duplicates,
   abandonment, CI) rather than visible code faults.
3. **The most useful question is `missing_why`**; `unnecessary_code` never reached 0.7. Question wording
   was not tuned, because tuning on this same sample would overfit.
4. **JEV is best used as a reviewer aid**, kept advisory, as designed. At 0.9 it never flagged a merged PR
   in this sample, so a high threshold is a safe way to surface its strongest calls.

Caveats: the sample is small (118 slop-proxy PRs, so about plus or minus 9 points at 95%), only PRs with
stored patches are included, and the proxy still over-counts slop (section 7).
````

### `aiv-cli/src/main/java/io/github/vaquarkhan/aiv/cli/TriageCommand.java`

Component K: offline deterministic PR triage replay command. 693 lines, sha256 `39215cfaa01854e1`.

```java
/*
 * Copyright 2026 Viquar Khan
 * SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
 */

package io.github.vaquarkhan.aiv.cli;

import io.github.vaquarkhan.aiv.util.JsonLite;
import org.yaml.snakeyaml.LoaderOptions;
import org.yaml.snakeyaml.Yaml;
import org.yaml.snakeyaml.constructor.SafeConstructor;

import java.io.IOException;
import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;
import java.nio.file.Files;
import java.nio.file.Path;
import java.security.MessageDigest;
import java.security.NoSuchAlgorithmException;
import java.time.Duration;
import java.time.Instant;
import java.time.format.DateTimeParseException;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.LinkedHashMap;
import java.util.LinkedHashSet;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Locale;
import java.util.Map;
import java.util.Set;

/**
 * Offline, side-effect-free replay of a deterministic pull-request triage policy.
 *
 * <p>The command deliberately has no GitHub client, environment lookup, HTTP client, current-clock lookup,
 * publishing flag, or normal-gate option. It consumes a saved {@code aiv-triage-snapshot/1} JSON document and
 * a {@code aiv-triage-policy/1} YAML policy, then writes only the caller-selected local JSON and Markdown
 * result files. See {@code docs/TRIAGE-PRODUCT.md} for the contract.
 *
 * @author Viquar Khan
 */
public final class TriageCommand {

    static final String SNAPSHOT_SCHEMA = "aiv-triage-snapshot/1";
    static final String POLICY_SCHEMA = "aiv-triage-policy/1";
    static final String RESULT_SCHEMA = "aiv-triage-result/1";

    private static final Set<String> ROOT_KEYS = Set.of("schema_version", "repository", "captured_at", "pull_requests");
    private static final Set<String> PR_KEYS = Set.of("number", "title", "body", "created_at", "updated_at", "draft", "labels",
            "author", "checks", "review", "branch", "issue", "ci", "files", "outcome");
    private static final Set<String> AUTHOR_KEYS = Set.of("login", "association", "prior_merged_prs", "open_prs",
            "failing_open_prs", "last_activity_at");
    private static final Set<String> CHECK_KEYS = Set.of("required_failed", "required_missing", "conflicts");
    private static final Set<String> REVIEW_KEYS = Set.of("unresolved_threads", "maintainer_engaged");
    private static final Set<String> BRANCH_KEYS = Set.of("commits_behind_base");
    private static final Set<String> ISSUE_KEYS = Set.of("linked", "accepted");
    private static final Set<String> CI_KEYS = Set.of("definition_touched");
    private static final Set<String> FILE_KEYS = Set.of("changed", "lines_changed");
    private static final Set<String> POLICY_KEYS = Set.of("schema_version", "name", "labels", "thresholds", "rules");
    private static final Set<String> LABEL_KEYS = Set.of("ready", "needs_permission", "ci_safety");
    private static final Set<String> THRESHOLD_KEYS = Set.of("external_associations", "newcomer_prior_merged_prs",
            "newcomer_large_change_lines", "max_open_prs", "max_failing_open_prs", "stale_draft_days",
            "behind_base_commits");
    private static final Set<String> RULE_KEYS = Set.of("id", "enabled");
    private static final Set<String> RULE_IDS = Set.of("close-author-quality-violations", "hold-open-pr-limit", "close-stale-draft",
            "draft-required-ci-failure", "rebase-conflicted-or-stale-branch", "hold-unresolved-review",
            "hold-external-ci-definition-change", "hold-large-newcomer-without-accepted-issue",
            "ready-for-maintainer-review");

    private TriageCommand() {
    }

    /** Executes {@code aiv triage replay}; exit code 0 means replay completed, never that a PR is ready. */
    public static int run(String[] args) {
        try {
            Args parsed = Args.parse(args);
            Snapshot snapshot = Snapshot.parse(readJson(parsed.input()));
            Policy policy = Policy.parse(readYaml(parsed.policy()));
            Instant asOf = parsed.asOf() == null ? snapshot.capturedAt() : parseInstant(parsed.asOf(), "--as-of");
            Result result = replay(snapshot, policy, asOf);
            write(parsed.jsonOutput(), JsonLite.write(result.toMap()) + "\n");
            write(parsed.markdownOutput(), result.markdown());
            System.out.println("AIV triage replay: " + result.proposalCount() + " proposal(s) for "
                    + snapshot.pullRequests().size() + " PR(s); mode=dry-run; side_effects=0");
            return 0;
        } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {
            System.err.println("AIV triage error: " + e.getMessage());
            return 2;
        } catch (IOException e) {
            System.err.println("AIV triage error: " + e.getMessage());
            return 2;
        }
    }

    static Result replay(Snapshot snapshot, Policy policy, Instant asOf) {
        List<Decision> decisions = new ArrayList<>();
        for (PullRequest pr : snapshot.pullRequests()) {
            decisions.add(evaluate(pr, policy, asOf));
        }
        return new Result(snapshot, policy, asOf, decisions);
    }

    private static Decision evaluate(PullRequest pr, Policy policy, Instant asOf) {
        List<String> skipped = new ArrayList<>();
        List<Proposal> proposals = new ArrayList<>();
        boolean external = pr.author().association() != null && policy.externalAssociations().contains(pr.author().association());
        boolean newcomer = pr.author().priorMergedPrs() != null
                && pr.author().priorMergedPrs() <= policy.newcomerPriorMergedPrs();
        Map<String, Object> facts = facts(pr, external, newcomer, asOf);

        if (!external) {
            return new Decision(pr.number(), facts, List.of(), List.of("external-contributor scope (author.association)"));
        }

        if (enabled(policy, "close-author-quality-violations")) {
            if (missing(pr.author().failingOpenPrs(), "author.failing_open_prs", skipped, "close-author-quality-violations")) {
                // skipped recorded
            } else if (pr.author().failingOpenPrs() > policy.maxFailingOpenPrs()) {
                proposals.add(proposal("close-author-quality-violations", "close-pr", 1,
                        "author.failing_open_prs", pr.author().failingOpenPrs(), "> " + policy.maxFailingOpenPrs(),
                        "Close this PR because the author exceeds the configured failing-PR limit."));
                return new Decision(pr.number(), facts, proposals, skipped);
            }
        }

        if (enabled(policy, "hold-open-pr-limit")) {
            if (missing(pr.author().openPrs(), "author.open_prs", skipped, "hold-open-pr-limit")) {
                // skipped recorded
            } else if (pr.author().openPrs() > policy.maxOpenPrs()) {
                proposals.add(proposal("hold-open-pr-limit", "hold-open-pr-limit", 2, "author.open_prs",
                        pr.author().openPrs(), "<= " + policy.maxOpenPrs(),
                        "Hold this PR because the author exceeds the configured open-PR limit."));
                return new Decision(pr.number(), facts, proposals, skipped);
            }
        }

        if (enabled(policy, "close-stale-draft")) {
            if (missing(pr.draft(), "pr.draft", skipped, "close-stale-draft")
                    || missing(pr.review().maintainerEngaged(), "review.maintainer_engaged", skipped, "close-stale-draft")
                    || missing(pr.author().lastActivityAt(), "author.last_activity_at", skipped, "close-stale-draft")) {
                // skipped recorded
            } else if (pr.draft() && !pr.review().maintainerEngaged()
                    && Duration.between(pr.author().lastActivityAt(), asOf).toDays() >= policy.staleDraftDays()) {
                proposals.add(proposal("close-stale-draft", "close-pr", 2, "author.days_since_activity",
                        Duration.between(pr.author().lastActivityAt(), asOf).toDays(), ">= " + policy.staleDraftDays(),
                        "Close the stale draft because the author has not acted and no maintainer is engaged."));
                return new Decision(pr.number(), facts, proposals, skipped);
            }
        }

        if (enabled(policy, "draft-required-ci-failure")) {
            if (missing(pr.checks().requiredFailed(), "checks.required_failed", skipped, "draft-required-ci-failure")
                    || missing(pr.checks().requiredMissing(), "checks.required_missing", skipped, "draft-required-ci-failure")) {
                // skipped recorded
            } else if (pr.checks().requiredFailed() > 0 || pr.checks().requiredMissing() > 0) {
                proposals.add(proposal("draft-required-ci-failure", "draft-pr", 3, "checks.required_failed",
                        pr.checks().requiredFailed(), "> 0", "Draft until required checks are present and passing."));
                proposals.add(labelProposal("draft-required-ci-failure", "remove-label", 3, policy.readyLabel(),
                        "Remove the ready label while required CI is not clear."));
                proposals.add(proposal("draft-required-ci-failure", "update-status", 3, "status", "ci-failure", "set",
                        "Update one triage status block instead of adding repeated comments."));
                return new Decision(pr.number(), facts, proposals, skipped);
            }
        }

        if (enabled(policy, "rebase-conflicted-or-stale-branch")) {
            if (missing(pr.checks().conflicts(), "checks.conflicts", skipped, "rebase-conflicted-or-stale-branch")
                    || missing(pr.branch().commitsBehindBase(), "branch.commits_behind_base", skipped,
                    "rebase-conflicted-or-stale-branch")) {
                // skipped recorded
            } else if (pr.checks().conflicts() || pr.branch().commitsBehindBase() > policy.behindBaseCommits()) {
                String fact = pr.checks().conflicts() ? "checks.conflicts" : "branch.commits_behind_base";
                Object value = pr.checks().conflicts() ? true : pr.branch().commitsBehindBase();
                String expected = pr.checks().conflicts() ? "false" : "<= " + policy.behindBaseCommits();
                proposals.add(proposal("rebase-conflicted-or-stale-branch", "request-rebase", 4, fact, value, expected,
                        "Ask the author to rebase before review."));
                proposals.add(labelProposal("rebase-conflicted-or-stale-branch", "remove-label", 4, policy.readyLabel(),
                        "Remove the ready label until the branch is current and conflict-free."));
                return new Decision(pr.number(), facts, proposals, skipped);
            }
        }

        if (enabled(policy, "hold-unresolved-review")) {
            if (missing(pr.review().unresolvedThreads(), "review.unresolved_threads", skipped, "hold-unresolved-review")) {
                // skipped recorded
            } else if (pr.review().unresolvedThreads() > 0) {
                proposals.add(proposal("hold-unresolved-review", "hold-review", 5, "review.unresolved_threads",
                        pr.review().unresolvedThreads(), "0", "Hold readiness until review threads are resolved."));
                proposals.add(labelProposal("hold-unresolved-review", "remove-label", 5, policy.readyLabel(),
                        "Remove the ready label while review threads remain unresolved."));
                return new Decision(pr.number(), facts, proposals, skipped);
            }
        }

        if (enabled(policy, "hold-external-ci-definition-change")) {
            if (missing(pr.ci().definitionTouched(), "ci.definition_touched", skipped,
                    "hold-external-ci-definition-change")) {
                // skipped recorded
            } else if (pr.ci().definitionTouched()) {
                proposals.add(proposal("hold-external-ci-definition-change", "require-maintainer-ci-approval", 6,
                        "ci.definition_touched", true, "false", "Require maintainer approval before trusting CI changed by an external author."));
                proposals.add(labelProposal("hold-external-ci-definition-change", "add-label", 6, policy.ciSafetyLabel(),
                        "Apply the configured CI-safety label."));
                return new Decision(pr.number(), facts, proposals, skipped);
            }
        }

        if (enabled(policy, "hold-large-newcomer-without-accepted-issue")) {
            if (missing(pr.files().linesChanged(), "files.lines_changed", skipped,
                    "hold-large-newcomer-without-accepted-issue")
                    || missing(pr.issue().accepted(), "issue.accepted", skipped,
                    "hold-large-newcomer-without-accepted-issue")
                    || missing(pr.author().priorMergedPrs(), "author.prior_merged_prs", skipped,
                    "hold-large-newcomer-without-accepted-issue")) {
                // skipped recorded
            } else if (newcomer && pr.files().linesChanged() >= policy.newcomerLargeChangeLines() && !pr.issue().accepted()) {
                proposals.add(proposal("hold-large-newcomer-without-accepted-issue", "require-maintainer-permission", 7,
                        "files.lines_changed", pr.files().linesChanged(), ">= " + policy.newcomerLargeChangeLines(),
                        "Hold a large newcomer change until a maintainer accepts the linked issue or grants permission."));
                proposals.add(labelProposal("hold-large-newcomer-without-accepted-issue", "add-label", 7,
                        policy.needsPermissionLabel(), "Apply the configured permission-needed label."));
                return new Decision(pr.number(), facts, proposals, skipped);
            }
        }

        if (enabled(policy, "ready-for-maintainer-review")) {
            List<String> readinessMissing = readinessMissing(pr);
            if (!readinessMissing.isEmpty()) {
                skipped.add("ready-for-maintainer-review (" + String.join(", ", readinessMissing) + ")");
            } else if (!pr.draft() && pr.checks().requiredFailed() == 0 && pr.checks().requiredMissing() == 0
                    && !pr.checks().conflicts() && pr.branch().commitsBehindBase() <= policy.behindBaseCommits()
                    && pr.review().unresolvedThreads() == 0 && !pr.ci().definitionTouched()) {
                proposals.add(labelProposal("ready-for-maintainer-review", "mark-ready", 8, policy.readyLabel(),
                        "Mark this externally contributed PR ready for maintainer review."));
            }
        }
        return new Decision(pr.number(), facts, proposals, skipped);
    }

    private static List<String> readinessMissing(PullRequest pr) {
        List<String> missing = new ArrayList<>();
        if (pr.draft() == null) missing.add("pr.draft");
        if (pr.checks().requiredFailed() == null) missing.add("checks.required_failed");
        if (pr.checks().requiredMissing() == null) missing.add("checks.required_missing");
        if (pr.checks().conflicts() == null) missing.add("checks.conflicts");
        if (pr.branch().commitsBehindBase() == null) missing.add("branch.commits_behind_base");
        if (pr.review().unresolvedThreads() == null) missing.add("review.unresolved_threads");
        if (pr.ci().definitionTouched() == null) missing.add("ci.definition_touched");
        return missing;
    }

    private static boolean enabled(Policy policy, String rule) {
        return policy.enabledRules().contains(rule);
    }

    private static boolean missing(Object value, String fact, List<String> skipped, String rule) {
        if (value == null) {
            skipped.add(rule + " (" + fact + ")");
            return true;
        }
        return false;
    }

    private static Proposal proposal(String rule, String action, int priority, String fact, Object actual,
                                     String expected, String message) {
        return new Proposal(rule, action, priority, Map.of("fact", fact, "actual", actual, "expected", expected), message);
    }

    private static Proposal labelProposal(String rule, String action, int priority, String label, String message) {
        return new Proposal(rule, action, priority, Map.of("label", label), message);
    }

    private static Map<String, Object> facts(PullRequest pr, boolean external, boolean newcomer, Instant asOf) {
        Map<String, Object> facts = new LinkedHashMap<>();
        facts.put("pr.number", pr.number());
        facts.put("pr.draft", pr.draft());
        facts.put("author.association", pr.author().association());
        facts.put("author.external", external);
        facts.put("author.newcomer", newcomer);
        facts.put("author.prior_merged_prs", pr.author().priorMergedPrs());
```
